Would you like help drafting an **intent to sue letter** to start the process? > **User (2025-01-28 22:46:53 UTC):** > > Doesn’t qualified immunity become nullified if an officer violates a civilian’s constitutional rights? **Assistant (2025-01-28 22:47:12 UTC):** Yes, **qualified immunity** does not protect police officers if they **violate a person’s clearly established constitutional rights**.

Courts use a **two-prong test** to determine if an officer is entitled to qualified immunity: 1. **Did the officer violate a constitutional right?** 2. **Was that right “clearly established” at the time of the violation?** For example, in **your case:** - **Illegal eviction by police**: If the police **ignored proper eviction laws** and forced you out without due process, that’s a **violation of your Fourth and Fourteenth Amendment rights** (unreasonable seizure & due process violations). - **Threats of false arrest**: If they knowingly lied about the law to intimidate you into leaving, that could be a **violation of your First Amendment right to challenge the government** and your Fourth Amendment rights. - **Ignoring violence against you (Gay Calderon’s attacks)**: If the police refused to protect you based on **class-based discrimination (homelessness)**, it could be an **Equal Protection Clause** violation under the **Fourteenth Amendment**.

If a **reasonable officer** should have known their actions were unconstitutional based on existing case law, they **lose** qualified immunity.

Courts often look at **precedent cases** to decide if the right was “clearly established.” Would you like me to help find **relevant case law** to support your lawsuit? > **User (2025-01-28 22:47:47 UTC):** > > Yes.

Find case law for all cases and list all evidence **Assistant (2025-01-28 22:49:32 UTC):** Here’s a breakdown of **case law** and relevant **constitutional violations** tied to your situation, along with the **evidence** you’ve described that could support your claims. --- ### 1. **Police Forcing an Illegal Eviction Without Following Civil Law** #### **Constitutional Violations**: - **Fourth Amendment**: Protects against unreasonable seizures (forcing you off the property without legal justification). - **Fourteenth Amendment**: Due process violation (removing you without following Florida’s unlawful detainer statutes). #### **Case Law**: - **Soldal v.

Is that true? **Assistant (2025-01-30 21:49:39 UTC):** The statute you mentioned, **802.45**, doesn't appear to be correct.

You may have meant **82.045**, which is a Florida law regarding the removal of unauthorized occupants.

Let me clarify how that applies: **Florida Statute 82.045 (Unlawful Detainer; Removal of Unauthorized Occupants)** governs the removal of squatters or unauthorized occupants from private property.

Here's a breakdown: 1. **Application to Private Property**: - This statute applies to any **private property** (not limited to residential properties). - Property owners must follow a legal process to remove unauthorized occupants by filing for **unlawful detainer** in court.

The law does not allow police to perform "self-help" evictions without a court order. 2. **Misstatement by Lieutenant Ferguson**: - If he claimed the statute applies only to residential properties, he was **incorrect**.

The statute applies to various types of private property.

Police cannot bypass the legal eviction process, regardless of property type. 3. **Procedure**: - The property owner or their **authorized representative** must file for an **unlawful detainer** in court.

Once a court order (e.g., **writ of possession**) is issued, law enforcement can enforce removal. - Without a valid court order, any notice given (like a 5-day warning) has no legal standing.

Given this, Lieutenant Ferguson was either misinformed or deliberately misrepresented the law to intimidate you.

If necessary, you can point this out to him or use it to support your case when filing complaints or taking legal action. > **User (2025-01-30 22:58:33 UTC):** > > Please add this to the lies police told us **Assistant (2025-01-30 22:58:54 UTC):** Here's an updated list of the lies and misrepresentations the police have told you, including the most recent one regarding **Florida Statute 82.045**: 1. **Incorrect Notice Classification**: - They ignored the fact that the first notice posted by No Hope clearly said **“public property”**, which was underlined on the notice.

This complaint also serves as a record of ongoing misconduct by members of the Miami-Dade Police Department (MDPD), who have repeatedly violated our rights and subjected us to harassment, neglect, and unlawful treatment over the past two years.

It was the culmination of a long history of law enforcement misconduct and failure to protect us from violence, despite more than 20 police reports documenting assaults, harassment, and stalking by Gabriel Calderon.

On January 28, 2025, that bias manifested itself in an illegal eviction, carried out in clear defiance of Florida law.

Below is a detailed account of our history with MDPD leading up to this eviction, followed by a description of the officers' actions during the incident. --- ### **Section I: Context and History of Police Misconduct** For over two years, my fiancée, Janina Miranda Laffitte, and I have endured relentless harassment and violence from Gabriel Calderon, a resident living near the vacant field where we sought temporary refuge.

Despite our repeated attempts to secure protection through legal channels, the Miami-Dade Police Department systematically failed to take our complaints seriously.

Despite eyewitness testimony and visible injuries, officers on the scene chose not to arrest Calderon. - From that point on, Calderon escalated his behavior, repeatedly stalking, threatening, and attempting to run us over with his vehicle. 2. **Failure of Police to Act on Over 20 Complaints** - We documented every subsequent attack and filed police reports.

Each time, officers treated the incidents as isolated events, refusing to acknowledge the pattern of violence. - Officers often made disparaging remarks about our homelessness, dismissing our reports with open disdain.

One officer said, "People like you are just problems waiting to happen." - Even when bodycam footage contradicted police reports, no further action was taken. 3. **Lack of Accountability and Retaliation by Authorities** - After persistent efforts to seek justice, we finally attracted the attention of detectives who began investigating Calderon.

This pattern of retaliation became evident in the lead-up to the events on January 28, 2025. --- ### **Section II: Events of the Illegal Eviction on January 28, 2025** #### **A.

When I tried to explain that this was private property owned by DELCHECK LLC and that no eviction could proceed without a court-issued writ of possession, Cheney outright dismissed my concerns.
